# 101: Is This the Power of Psychology? (BONUS)

For 1100 powerstones.

----------

Roxanne took a deep breath.

This was Mr. Johan, after all.

She recalled Graveler and spoke softly to its Poké Ball, "It's okay, we'll keep working at it. Someday…"

After putting away Graveler's Poké Ball, Roxanne didn't seem discouraged. She brought out her second one.

Regaining her focus, she tossed the next ball into the arena.

"Let's go, Probopass!"

Probopass, the Compass Pokémon, a pure Rock type. Although it was Rock-type, its magnetic nature also made it adept with Electric-type moves.

From what Johan knew, Probopass was Roxanne's main Pokémon.

The battle resumed.

"Probopass, Sandstorm!"

Roxanne commanded swiftly.

A faint yellow light shimmered over Probopass as a wave of sand began to sweep across the battlefield, gradually filling the entire arena.

At the same time, Johan's Pokédex displayed Probopass's details.

[Probopass]

Type: Rock

Ability: Sturdy

Moves Known: Tackle, Harden, Block, Rock Throw, Thunder Wave, Rest, Spark, Rock Slide…

Mood: …

Seeing the moves Probopass knew, Johan's mind started working.

A "sandstorm team," also known as a "sandstorm lineup," relies primarily on sandstorm weather.

However, the best setup for a "sandstorm team" usually involves having a Pokémon with the Sand Stream ability, which triggers a sandstorm immediately upon entering the battlefield.

For example, Johan's Vulpix has the Snow Warning ability, which changes the weather to snow as soon as it enters battle.

This way, they gain an advantage right from the start.

But with Roxanne's Probopass, which has to use the move Sandstorm to summon the weather, they've gained the weather effect, sure, but at the cost of losing precious momentum.

Johan smiled slightly.

"Haunter, Spite."

Probopass, busy summoning the sandstorm, suddenly felt a tap on its back. It whipped around, but saw nothing.

And as it turned back with confusion…

It was met with a ghastly face with a metallic shell—a face that looked like Metagross's terrifying visage!

"Ka-kah!!"

Probopass shuddered instantly, feeling as if its heart had skipped a beat.

Spite needs to catch opponents off guard to work best—classic psychological tactics.

But Johan had modified this approach slightly.

"Spite" should not only surprise but also target the opponent's most vulnerable spot—"Psychological Warfare: Enhanced Version."

Seeing Probopass's reaction, Roxanne frowned.

When had Haunter moved so close to Probopass?

And did Mr. Johan really know Pokémon so well?

To think he even knew that Probopass feared Metagross most…

In the wild, Probopass feared Metagross above all else because Metagross was drawn to Probopass's magnetic stones, practically its natural predator.

Also, Haunter's transformation ability—it's a bit too much, isn't it?

Anyone who didn't know better would think it was a Ditto!

"Confuse Ray."

Taking advantage of Probopass's flinch, Haunter immediately conjured a glowing orb of strange light.

The orb hit Probopass directly, and soon it was engulfed in confusion.

All for a single sandstorm—the sluggish Probopass had lost a lot of ground, which made the trade-off seem hardly worth it.

Roxanne's face was tense, but she continued to command calmly.

"Probopass, Thunder Wave!"

"Confusion" doesn't prevent moves entirely; there's still about a one-third chance of self-inflicted harm.

Whether it was Roxanne's calmness or sheer luck, Probopass managed to use its move undeterred.

Sparks of golden electricity crackled along Probopass's surface.

If the Thunder Wave paralyzed Haunter, then the tables might turn.

However…

Since Johan had already seen which moves Probopass knew, it wasn't hard to predict what Roxanne might do next.

"Haunter, slip into Probopass's shadow."

As soon as Roxanne gave her command, Johan's voice followed.

"Ka-kah~~"

Golden electric arcs scattered everywhere, but Haunter had already slid into Probopass's shadow and wasn't affected.

After all, not everyone thinks to target their own shadow.

"A perfect read? Dodging Thunder Wave by hiding in the shadow… Now Roxanne and Probopass are in trouble."

"Why do I feel like this Pokémon psychology teacher is really strong…"

"Be more confident—take out the 'feel like' part; he's clearly strong. Didn't you see Roxanne's expression?"

"…"

As the spectators continued to whisper, Johan issued the next command before Roxanne could respond:

"Lick."

Haunter popped out of Probopass's shadow with a gleeful "ka-kah" and licked Probopass's back as the golden electric arcs faded away.

Probopass was instantly affected by paralysis.

"Toxic."

"Will-O-Wisp."

"Hex…"

The crowd fell silent.

Somewhere in the stands, someone suddenly shivered and began rubbing their arms, covered in goosebumps.

"Whoa! Flinch, confusion, paralysis, burn, poison… This Haunter is way too 'dirty'…"

"Not just that! Didn't you notice Haunter's approach to Spite? And those precise reads… Is this the power of psychology?"

"Poor Probopass… getting pummeled physically and mentally."

"But why didn't Probopass use Rest?"

"Wait, if he didn't switch Pokémon, does he plan to take on all three at once?!"

"…"

Even the referee couldn't help but pat his own arm, trying to shake off the chills as he announced in a stiff tone:

"P-Probopass is unable to battle!"

With so many negative effects stacked on it, no matter how strong Probopass was, it couldn't withstand that kind of onslaught. Its Sturdy ability had become practically useless in this match.

"If Roxanne had gone with Rest, the fight would've been over. Johan would've let Haunter max out its Nasty Plot boosts, and the next round wouldn't even be necessary."

Cynthia muttered quietly with a slight smile, only loud enough for Diantha beside her to hear.

Diantha chuckled in agreement.

"With all those negative effects, Haunter chose not to use Hypnosis or keep stacking Nasty Plot. Maybe Johan was worried about Probopass using Sleep Talk. If it hit Explosion... the resulting blast of rock fragments and sand would be difficult for Haunter to dodge."

"Quite a… surprisingly cautious move."

Their insight went beyond what the average spectator could see, allowing them to deduce some of the finer points of the battle between Johan and Roxanne.

Cynthia continued, "But Roxanne risking Probopass to set up Sandstorm… Is she that confident in her last Pokémon?"

A hint of concern flashed in her eyes.

Diantha glanced sideways at Cynthia, smiling subtly.

Meanwhile…

Johan called Haunter over.

"Roxanne's next Pokémon will most likely be the original Gym Leader's Pokémon. She was willing to lose Probopass just to set up Sandstorm, so it's probably a Sand Rush Pokémon."

"It's either Sandslash or Excadrill, and we'll…"

"Ka-kah?" Haunter blinked in confusion.

Excadrill?

What's that?

A picture of Garchomp popped into its mind, but with a Raticate's head.

"Uh, my bad," Johan coughed, realizing his slip. "It's Excadrill. But I'm guessing it's Sandslash. Still, we'll adapt our strategy based on what comes out."

On the other side of the field…

Roxanne's expression grew complex.

Was she really going to put her hopes on her father's Pokémon?

This wasn't what she wanted.

But as the Gym Leader, it was her duty to assess challengers thoroughly. That's what being a Gym Leader was all about!

# 102: Seriously, You Should Be Called ‘Vortex Haunter’ from Now On! (BONUS)

Bonus for 1200 powerstones. I'm going to stop doing each 100 powerstones indefinitely, I'm tired.

-------------

"Let's go, Sandslash!"

"Kucha!"

Taking a deep breath, Roxanne threw her last Poké Ball. A Pokémon with a tawny body covered in sharp spines and a fierce gaze appeared on the field.

Sandslash, the Ground-type Mouse Pokémon.

Just as Johan expected.

Upon seeing the Pokémon, Johan mentally noted this with a bit of relief—thankfully, it wasn't Excadrill. Excadrill, with its Ground and Steel types, would have been a much harder challenge.

"Sandslash? Isn't that the Gym Leader's Pokémon?"

"This match is really worth it! It's been so long since I've seen a battle like this…"

The audience's excitement rose as they watched the battle unfold.

Seeing Sandslash on the field, some of Rustboro Gym's regulars couldn't help but chime in.

Ordinarily, they'd never get the chance to see the Gym Leader's main Pokémon in action.

As soon as Sandslash took the field, part of the swirling sandstorm began to wrap around its body.

Sand Rush ability!

[Sand Rush: Pokémon with this ability have double speed during a sandstorm]

The battle continued.

"Sandslash, remember, don't look Haunter in the eyes—start moving!"

Roxanne reminded it carefully.

Don't look Haunter in the eyes?

Johan's expression turned slightly amused, but he said nothing further.

Sandslash began running swiftly around the field, which had now been layered with thick yellow sand. As it ran, it dragged its claws through the sandy terrain, kicking up even more dust.

"Swords Dance!"

"Kucha!"

As it moved, Sandslash's gaze sharpened, a light white aura gathering around it, forming phantom swords that circled it with a metallic clanging sound.

Roxanne's plan was clear: to strengthen Sandslash with Swords Dance, then use its boosted Sand Rush speed under the sandstorm to deal with Haunter as quickly as possible.

"Time isn't on Roxanne's side. She still has to take on two other Pokémon, and the sandstorm won't last forever…"

"Still, judging by the setup, her Probopass probably had an item like the Smooth Rock to extend the sandstorm."

Seeing Sandslash's data in his Pokédex, Johan made his own counterplay.

"Haunter, Toxic."

A sticky, toxic liquid quickly formed and began oozing from Haunter.

"Dig, dodge it!"

Sandslash vanished from sight in a burst of earth and stone, leaving a dark hole behind and a trail of upturned dirt.

Johan smirked.

"Aim for where the ground is raised."

"Ka-kah~~"

Haunter grinned, lobbing Toxic at the path.

The deep purple liquid quickly spread out, covering nearly half the field and even seeping underground.

Soon, little bubbles of purple began popping up in various places, creating a swampy field of poison.

The crowd gasped collectively at the sight.

"Whoa—this is Toxic?"

"That looks like it could be a poison trap!"

Haunter cackled gleefully at the stunned onlookers.

"Please, I slurp Toxic Orbs like candy! Johan calls it my 'divine tongue'!"

Boom!

Suddenly, the ground beneath Haunter erupted. Sandslash, its body already tinged with a purple hue, shot out, its eyes fierce and claws coated with a spiraling dark mist.

Shadow Claw!

"Protect."

Johan and Haunter were ready.

A green barrier appeared just as Haunter raised its hands.

Crack—

Sandslash's attack shattered Protect with ease.

"Sandslash, Rock Tomb, then hit it with Shadow Claw again!"

Roxanne clenched her fists tightly as she gave the command.

With the boost from Swords Dance, and Dark-type Shadow Claw's high critical hit rate, if Sandslash hit Haunter, it could very well be a one-hit knockout!

Though its first attempt failed, Sandslash pressed its claws into the ground, summoning chains of rocks of various sizes.

"Substitute."

Bzzzz—

Haunter's body shivered slightly as the rocky chains passed right through it.

Using Substitute to dodge?

Inevitably, Sandslash's claws struck the Substitute!

"I remember him now! He's that Johan guy who used 'Substitute Toxic Protect' to beat the 'Dragon Master' Lance!"

Witnessing this, one spectator, who had felt Johan seemed familiar, finally recognized him.

"No wonder he looked familiar—was he setting up the 'Substitute Toxic Protect' strategy all along?"

"No wonder Roxanne is playing so cautiously…"

"Wait! Since when could 'Substitute' counterattack?!"

The action on the field moved too fast to follow.

Suddenly, someone realized that as Sandslash hit the Substitute, it also got punched back by it!

What's going on?

Johan himself looked momentarily puzzled.

But when he saw that Haunter's Pokédex entry now listed Night Shade as a move, he understood.

Did it just learn a new move mid-battle?

Did Haunter just embed Night Shade into its Substitute?

No way.

Did it just pull off Shadow Clone Jutsu?!

Although things hadn't gone entirely as expected, Johan wasn't about to let an opening like this slip by when Sandslash was forced to fall back.

"Haunter, Shadow Ball!"

"Ka-kah!!"

Haunter, clearly in high spirits, summoned a shadowy black orb between its hands.

But this time, the Shadow Ball took a bit longer to form, and its size was noticeably bigger.

Roxanne's eyes widened as she called out, "Sandslash, dig quickly!"

Just recovering from its unexpected hit by the Substitute, Sandslash glanced up to see the enormous Shadow Ball Haunter was charging, and it gulped audibly.

It immediately started digging.

However…

With a triumphant "Ka-kah," Haunter launched the Shadow Ball—which was now at least three times larger than usual—down toward Sandslash.

Boom!!

The ground shook, leaving a crater and scattering debris in every direction.

From the audience came a wave of surprised shouts.

"That's a Shadow Ball?!"

"That's a supercharged Shadow Ball!"

"I expected 'Substitute Toxic Protect' to be the main strategy, but who knew… there were even bigger surprises!"

"…"

Johan's eye twitched slightly.

Shadow Clone Jutsu?

A mega-sized Shadow Ball?

Not to mention Haunter Transformation…

"You know what, Haunter? You should be called 'Vortex Haunter' from now on."

In the corner of the stands, both Cynthia and Diantha looked stunned.

Layering Ghost-type energy from Shadow Claw onto Shadow Ball?

After a moment, Diantha pursed her lips. "On our way home, Gardevoir told me that after training with Haunter, she'd sensed something terrifying within its potential. She 'foresaw' a vision of Haunter dancing with a monstrous dragon…"

Cynthia nodded, recovering from her surprise.

"I remember Johan mentioning that Haunter had slept for a thousand years before becoming his Pokémon…"

Indeed, all living beings seem to cross paths with other lives, creating bonds that give rise to the most unpredictable outcomes.

However…

Haunter might have immense potential, but it's only with trust and understanding that such potential can be fully realized.

Cynthia thought back to the warmth she had seen in Johan's home when she visited.

Johan's bond with Haunter was as deep as her own with Garchomp.

Strength and levels are just a Pokémon's baseline. Their bond with their trainer determines their true potential. When trainer and Pokémon work as one, they're capable of incredible feats.

She, too, often achieved the unexpected alongside her Garchomp.

Reflecting on this, she nodded again.

"It's no wonder Garchomp and Lucario don't feel uncomfortable around Haunter."

Especially Lucario, who usually kept to itself.

Maybe it had sensed something through its mastery of Aura.

"S-Sandslash is unable to battle! The winner is… Challenger Johan!!"

The referee, snapping out of his daze, raised both flags and announced the result.

Roxanne exhaled a long sigh of relief, feeling a surprising weight lift from her shoulders.

With a complex expression, she looked at Johan.

This was… the power of Mr. Johan.

Roxanne gathered herself and walked over to Johan.

"Ka-kah, ka-kah~~ヾ(▼▼;)"

Haunter was waving at the audience with a smug grin.

"How are we supposed to re-challenge the Gym now with that kind of showboating?" Johan whispered sternly.

"Ka… Ka-kah! Σ(дlll)"

Oh, right!

Haunter suddenly froze as realization hit.

Roxanne, who was just about to present the Gym Badge, overheard Johan's remark.

Roxanne: "…"

Her earlier frustration at losing vanished instantly.

"Congratulations, Mr. Johan," she said with a sly tone.

Johan cleared his throat, looking slightly embarrassed.

"Ahem, well… actually, you did great, Roxanne."

It was true.

Realizing Haunter's strength, she had strategically used Probopass as a decoy to set up a new plan, combining that with her powerful Sandslash. Her tactical approach had been flawless.

However, Sandslash wasn't her own Pokémon, so there was a slight

lag in her commands.

Roxanne bowed respectfully.

"Thank you. Sir, here is the Stone Badge, along with the Rock Tomb and Dig TMs."

I'll need to work harder from here. Our tactical gap is still too wide.

As Johan accepted the badge, he felt a bit emotional.

Was this his first badge?

Had he begun the path to the "Ever Grande Conference"?

And he'd scored two free TMs, too, which saved a decent amount of money.

Haunter, grinning ear to ear as it held the TMs, skipped along with Johan as they left the Gym.

Even though it couldn't use either move, that didn't stop it from feeling like it had just hit the jackpot.

"Congratulations, Johan!"

Outside the Gym, Cynthia and Diantha were already waiting.

"Um… Haunter…"

Diantha looked over at Haunter, her expression bemused.

"Haunter… why are you glowing?"

# 103: I am Giovanni

"Ka-kah, ka-kah, ka-kah~~"

In a restaurant not far from Rustboro Gym, Haunter was feasting with gusto, his previously pallid face visibly regaining its color.

"So full of energy," Diantha remarked, adjusting her sunglasses as she watched him.

Johan, however, glanced at the stacks of plates piling up on the table and couldn't help but wipe the sweat from his brow.

Full of energy?

Haunter had given Johan quite the scare when they'd left the Gym.

Without saying a word, he'd started turning pale.

For a moment, Johan thought Nosepass's magnetic field interference had somehow triggered a "trade evolution" condition for Haunter.

But on second thought, if Nosepass had interfered with Haunter's magnetism, the evolution would have happened mid-battle.

As it turned out, Haunter's pallor was just a side effect of using too much energy.

"Had me thinking he'd mutated," Johan muttered, slightly embarrassed.

"Mutation isn't likely, but evolution is possible," Cynthia suggested with a smile, stroking Vulpix's fur.

"If I remember correctly, Haunter requires a trade or a special magnetic field to evolve. Roxanne's Nosepass alone wouldn't be enough."

At the mention of evolution, Johan frowned a bit.

Haunter needed a trade or a magnetic field, and Vulpix needed an Ice Stone.

Noticing his thoughts, Cynthia spoke up again, "Professor Carolina has a communicator that Mr. Bill sent over. If you need it, I can put in a word for you."

Then she added, "Though the magnetic field isn't very stable, so you'll need some luck."

Johan remembered that communicators and Pokémon storage systems had been inventions pioneered by Bill. At this point in time, such devices probably weren't perfected yet.

Still, it was an option.

But if Haunter's evolution failed, the magnetic instability could end up harming him.

Once Haunter was full and had regained his strength, the three left the restaurant.

Cynthia then mentioned she and Diantha were heading for Sootopolis City.

"You'll be there, right?"

Counting the days, the "Youth Cup" would be starting soon.

Since it was an exhibition match, they needed to familiarize themselves with the venue and make an appearance.

"I think I'll go," Johan replied.

After all, his holiday was nearly over, and once school was back in session, he'd be busy with midterms.

As a teacher, he still hadn't even prepared the exam papers.

Though given his class schedule, sneaking away for a day or two shouldn't be an issue.

After all, he had three invitations. Not going would be quite rude.

In the end, Johan watched as Cynthia and Diantha took off on Garchomp.

He couldn't help feeling a bit envious. Having a Pokémon that could fly and carry passengers was quite convenient—no need to buy plane tickets.

When he got home, Johan sat down at his computer, ready to finalize the midterm exam.

His class was mostly theory-based, with a lot of content to memorize and review, so preparing the test was actually quite simple.

But before he got started, he took out the Stone Badge and the two TMs from Rustboro Gym and placed them on his desk.

"Gotta admit, they're pretty well-made," he mused, admiring the badge's quality—an official Hoenn League item had to be top-notch.

"Better keep it safe; wouldn't want Tinkatink trying to smash it. I might even get more badges down the line for a nice little collection…"

As for the two TMs…

"Gym TMs are definitely different from the ones from the department store or online… Wait, what's this?"

Johan noticed a small compartment in the TMs, which, when opened, revealed two tiny booklets.

He picked one up and opened it.

"To better master this move, pair it with Rock Incense, Hard Stone fragments…"

Snap!

He quickly closed the booklet.

Turning around, he saw Haunter and the others huddled together, whispering about something and not paying him any attention. Relieved, he opened the booklets again to skim them quickly, his heart sinking.

The Gym-issued TMs included instruction manuals!

Not only did they have insights from the Gym's personal understanding and usage of the move, but also advice on how to strengthen the move and maximize its power!

This was outrageous…

If he hadn't seen these, he might've been able to hold back. But now that he had… How could he resist making his Pokémon stronger?

And if he couldn't resist, his already-depleted bank account would take a serious hit!

Across the room…

"Chu!"

Back at home, Tinkatink snuck over to Haunter and Vulpix.

"Ka-kah?"

"Oowu?"

Both Haunter and Vulpix were always very patient and protective of their newly-hatched teammate.

With a look of mystery, Tinkatink beckoned them closer, gesturing for them to come in for a secret.

Haunter and Vulpix exchanged glances, then moved closer.

The three Pokémon formed a tight huddle, heads touching.

"Chu, Chu!"

Tada!

Tinkatink held out her hand, revealing a dark blue stone in her pink palm that seemed to emit a faint magnetic pull.

"Ka-kah?!"

Nosepass's stone?!

Recognizing it instantly, Haunter opened his mouth to exclaim but quickly clapped a hand over it.

Tinkatink looked proud, pointing at Haunter, then the stone.

"Chu!"

Her meaning was clear—didn't Haunter need a special magnetic field to evolve?

This stone was magnetic!

"Ka-kah~~ (ノ_<.)"

Haunter's eyes turned watery with emotion.

Tinkatink's instinct was to smash any metal she came across into her hammer. Yet she'd resisted the urge, saving the rare metal instead and bringing it home just for him.

"Chu?"

Tinkatink tilted her head, looking puzzled.

"Oowu?"

That's when Vulpix's serious voice sounded.

You… didn't steal it, did you?

They'd all seen Tinkatink's "pickpocket" ability in action.

Though her intentions were good, stealing wasn't a good habit. If Johan found out, Tinkatink would end up in big trouble.

Tinkatink quickly shook her head.

"Chu—Chu—"

She'd found it while the Gym staff was cleaning up, tossed aside as garbage.

They didn't want it… so it wasn't stealing, right?

All three Pokémon glanced over at Johan.

Seeing he was too busy with a tiny booklet to notice them, they let out a collective sigh of relief.

"Oowu…"

Probably not… Maybe it's… just recycling?

Of course, this stone alone didn't have enough magnetism to trigger Haunter's evolution—Haunter had tried licking Nosepass directly before.

But the gesture still touched him deeply.

He gave Tinkatink a pat on the head, signaling her to keep it for herself.

"Chu?"

Really?

Tinkatink blinked her big lavender eyes in surprise.

Getting approval from both Haunter and Vulpix, she excitedly took out her tiny hammer and began tinkering with the stone, clinking and clanking.

"Phew…"

Johan sighed with relief, stretching his back.

The first draft of the midterm exam was done, and he'd add to it as ideas came to him.

For now, it was a simple test. The questions were easy, covering only material from the book. As long as his students studied, they'd do just fine.

"Man, I really am a nice teacher…"

Turning around, he saw that the three Pokémon were behaving and not causing trouble, which improved his mood instantly.

"A peaceful day… this is nice."

He casually picked up his phone and started browsing comments online.

His popularity had dropped recently, so there weren't many new "clients" looking for advice, and the questions he got were all fairly simple.

But as he scrolled, one message caught his eye.

[Who Am I]: How can I meet…

Only half the message was there. Mewtwo's avatar was already grayed out, meaning he wasn't online.

Meet… who?

Meet a new friend? Or maybe Mewtwo No. 2?

Johan had always kept an eye on Mewtwo, this "troubled case," in particular.

But now that it was clearly under Team Rocket's control, it probably wasn't easy for it to get online, let alone reach out.

As Johan pondered ways to help Mewtwo learn to restrain its "Id," a new friend request popped up.

[King of the Earth]: Accept.

"Didn't this person try to add me before?"

Once again, Johan hit "Decline."

His profile stated clearly that anyone wanting help should specify their Pokémon's condition.

He'd had plenty of random friend requests in the past, but they'd mostly stopped as his popularity dipped.

This person was persistent.

Meanwhile, at the Team Rocket HQ beneath Viridian City in Kanto…

"Boss, we intercepted Mewtwo's communication, and we've managed to trace the other user's account," reported Matori, holding a tablet.

Her gaze lingered on the tray of carefully made Meowth-shaped cookies on Giovanni's desk, her eyes hopeful.

"Oh? What cut it off?"

Giovanni looked up from his files, noticing the cookies on his desk. He was feeling a bit peckish, so he grabbed one and bit down.

He'd never been picky about food.

Matori's cheeks turned pink as she tightened her fists

.

"Hmm?"

Seeing she hadn't answered, Giovanni turned her way.

Snapping back, Matori stammered, "I-it was a power outage. Someone pulled the lab's main breaker."

Giovanni nodded thoughtfully.

"So, it's that Grain in the Field user?"

"Y-yes."

"Give me the phone."

Taking the phone from Matori, Giovanni sent a friend request directly to "Grain in the Field."

Seconds later, he laughed.

He'd been declined.

Thinking a moment, he sent another request.

This time, he was clear about who he was.

[King of the Earth]: I am Giovanni.

Johan stared at the friend request notification on his screen, mind blank.

# 104: Do You Have Kids, Giovanni?

Who…?

Giovanni?!

Johan stared at the words on his screen, unable to process them for a long moment.

With such a straightforward approach, it couldn't possibly be an impersonator, could it?

The head of Team Rocket, Giovanni, was actually sending him a friend request? Most likely… because of Mewtwo? Should he accept it?

Anyone who has ever played the Pokémon games or watched the Pokémon anime knows Team Rocket, and they certainly know Giovanni, the infamous head of the organization. In fact, in the Pokémon Adventures manga, Giovanni's Beedrill even overpowered Lance, who was already one of the Elite Four at the time.

Team Rocket's influence is so vast across the Kanto and Johto regions that even the League doesn't dare act rashly against them.

And with Giovanni directly revealing his identity in the friend request…

"Hold on a minute. Giovanni should also still be the Gym Leader of Viridian Gym," Johan thought to himself.

As a Gym Leader, it made sense to add him as a friend. He had no reason to decline, right?

"Wait."

A sudden realization hit Johan.

"Did I… reject this friend request once already?"

He quickly looked around his room, feeling a strange sense of unease.

"Ka-kah?"

Noticing his movements, Haunter, hanging from the ceiling, looked over in curiosity.

"Haunter, nothing unusual around the house, right?" Johan asked.

"Ka-kah?"

Unusual?

Haunter looked puzzled, sticking his head into the ceiling for a moment, then floating around the room before re-emerging.

With a shrug, Haunter raised his hands.

"Ka-kah~~"

Nope, nothing out there.

Even Vulpix and Tinkatink looked at him inquisitively.

Johan breathed a sigh of relief.

"It's nothing," he said, waving them off.

Good—no surprise ninjas showing up.

Then he looked back at Haunter and added, "Haunter, if you ever see someone sneaking around here like a ninja, don't hesitate. Blast them with a strong move!"

"Ka-kah?"

A ninja?

With a mischievous laugh, Haunter clasped his claws together in a mock ninja hand sign.

Like this?

"Yes, exactly. If that happens, act fast, and make sure to hit hard," Johan instructed.

"Ka-kah~~"

Haunter pounded a fist to his chest, confidently assuring Johan that he could handle ten of them, no problem!

Johan just shook his head, choosing not to elaborate further.

After all, if a ninja ever did show up here, it would likely be Janine—the Fuchsia Gym Leader and future Poison-type Elite Four member of the Johto region!

As it happened, Janine was one of Team Rocket's three top executives.

Along with her were Sabrina and Lt. Surge—both of whom also held Gym Leader positions in Kanto.

And if rumors were to be believed, even Blaine from Cinnabar Island was…

Eight Gyms in the region, and five of them were aligned with Team Rocket—just goes to show their far-reaching influence.

Thinking it over, though, Giovanni didn't seem like the petty type. So what if he declined his friend request once?

After some thought, Johan decided to accept.

After all, Giovanni was still a Gym Leader, and he had openly stated his identity. Declining again could very well send Janine to his door!

Once he accepted, Johan felt much calmer.

Worst case… he'd just hide out in Sinnoh. The Sinnoh Galactic Team wasn't exactly soft, either.

And if he could stay at Cynthia's place, that'd be even safer.

Almost immediately, Giovanni sent him a message.

[King of the Earth]: Hello, Professor Johan.

Not bad.

He's rather polite.

Johan had some understanding of Giovanni's character through the anime and the Pokémon Adventures manga.

The Giovanni in the anime represented pure evil.

But his "evil" differed from the chaotic malice of villains like Archie and Maxie. Giovanni's actions followed a strict order and code, making him more a "Lawful Evil," if you had to categorize it.

In the Pokémon Adventures manga, Giovanni represented evil too, but he also displayed glimpses of warmth and humanity.

For instance, in the manga, Giovanni sought out Mewtwo while searching for his long-lost son.

He held his own Pokémon, especially Beedrill, with a loyalty and care that surpassed many Trainers.

Even Red, though he opposed Team Rocket, had a certain mutual respect for Giovanni—when Red was frozen in ice, Giovanni was the one who saved him.

In summary, Giovanni from the manga was, first and foremost, a father, and then the head of Team Rocket.

But regardless of the adaptation, Giovanni's goal remained consistent—

"If the League can rule year after year, why not me?"

That's how Team Rocket came to infiltrate the League to the point where five of Kanto's Gym Leaders were under its influence.

And one day, it could extend all the way to the Elite Four!

[Grain in the Field]: Hello, Gym Leader Giovanni. How can I assist you?

For now, Johan pretended to only recognize Giovanni as the Gym Leader of Viridian. It wouldn't do to acknowledge any other affiliations.

This way, whatever happened later couldn't be blamed on him.

[King of the Earth]: Professor Johan, you're very talented. Would you be interested in coming to Kanto to work for me?

An offer?

This was an offer, right?

The reason was probably obvious to Johan.

It had to do with Mewtwo.

[Grain in the Field]: I appreciate the offer, Gym Leader Giovanni, but as a teacher, I have a class of wonderful students to teach. I can't leave Hoenn at the moment.

He declined politely.

[King of the Earth]: A responsible teacher indeed.

[Grain in the Field]: Well, a teacher is a teacher for life, after all.

[King of the Earth]: I may have been a bit presumptuous.

Giovanni's courteousness was almost surprising to Johan.

Then again, it was that charisma that made people like Sabrina and Janine willing to serve him.

Just when Johan thought the matter was settled, Giovanni sent another message.

[King of the Earth]: In that case, would you be interested in being my personal consultant?

"Uh…"

Johan rubbed his temples, hesitating as he typed.

"I'm very flattered, but I don't think I can…"

Before he could finish, another message arrived.

[King of the Earth]: Of course, this comes with compensation. I'm certain it won't disappoint you, Professor.

Huh?

Johan looked at the half-finished reply, then hit backspace repeatedly.

Giovanni continued.

[King of the Earth]: And you wouldn't need to come to Viridian. We can communicate remotely.

A remote consultant role, with the client being a Gym Leader…

It all sounded quite reasonable?

[Grain in the Field]: What would my responsibilities be?

[King of the Earth]: You would be providing psychological counseling for me, my Pokémon, and perhaps even Pokémon that I am… acquainted with, including, as you know… Mewtwo!

At the mention of "Mewtwo," Johan felt a slight twinge.

Nobody else would know about Mewtwo's connection to Team Rocket, so he had no real excuse to decline.

And as for Mewtwo… Johan had taken the case and was committed to doing his best.

He couldn't just ignore Mewtwo, especially knowing the issues it faced.

Just then, a notification appeared.

[Your account has been credited with 3,000,000 Pokédollars. Remaining balance: 3,222,100 Pokédollars]

Thousands… tens of thousands… hundreds of thousands… millions…

Whoa.

Three million just like that?

More generous than Devon Corporation!

Wait a minute.

[Grain in the Field]: Gym Leader Giovanni…

[King of the Earth]: Given the work you've already done with Mewtwo, I went ahead and reimbursed you for those services.

[King of the Earth]: As for the future, we'll start with a salary of one million Pokédollars per month. How does that sound?

A million a month… that would mean…

Twelve million a year?!

Does Team Rocket really have that much money?

Oh, right—they're backed by Silph Co., with five gyms and three major squads… Well, that's different.

[Grain in the Field]: Gym Leader Giovanni, I haven't…

[King of the Earth]: Then it's settled!

[Grain in the Field]: Gym Leader Giovanni, I haven't agreed yet!

[King of the Earth]: Congratulations, Professor Johan. I look forward to a fruitful collaboration! (Handshake.jpg)

[Grain in the Field]: ???

Seriously?

Giovanni, you're ignoring my reply?

Is this coercion?

The funds were already in his account, and now he'd have a hard time explaining it all if anything happened…

Did this mean he was now officially Giovanni's personal consultant?

Well.

Johan decided to take a screenshot.

For evidence.

If this ever got out, he'd insist that he was coerced.

[King of the Earth]: Any other questions, Professor Johan?

Johan was speechless.

After a couple of deep breaths, he decided to ask the most important question.

[Grain in the Field]: This might be a bit personal, Gym Leader Giovanni, but… do you have children?

**[

King of the Earth]: ???**

It was crucial!

After all, Johan needed to determine if he was dealing with the Giovanni from the anime or the one from the manga.

The Giovanni from the anime was beyond saving, solely focused on taking down the League.

But if it was the manga's Giovanni… well, there might still be hope.

After a while, Giovanni replied.

[King of the Earth]: You're the first to ask me that question.

[King of the Earth]: The answer is… yes.

[King of the Earth]: But he's missing.

Johan clenched his fist.

All the clues fit!

Could there be a "Red" out there, too?

[Grain in the Field]: I'm certain you'll find him one day.

[King of the Earth]: Thank you.

After that, Giovanni's avatar dimmed, signaling he had gone offline.

Johan sat back in his chair, rubbing his chin.

"Really, I'm just consulting as a Gym Leader's Pokémon counselor…"

Without Giovanni, Mewtwo would still need help. He'd keep counseling it anyway.

"Looking at it that way, I'm practically earning a few million for free."

"Still… should I notify someone?"

With that in mind, Johan walked out onto the balcony.

"Hello, Drake? It's Johan…"

In Team Rocket's Viridian base.

Giovanni put down his phone with a long sigh, leaning back into his chair, a distant look in his eyes.

Standing nearby, Matori was surprised. She'd never seen her boss look so pensive.

Could this really be the Giovanni she knew, the one she thought was invincible?

Strangely enough… she felt a pang of sympathy.

So this is Professor Johan's psychological influence. How… terrifying.

With a small voice, she spoke up. "Boss, why not have a cookie to relax?"

"Mm."

Giovanni gave a slight nod, though he made no move to take one.

But that alone was enough to make Matori's heart race.

"This matter with 'Grain in the Field'… aside from you and me, no one else is to know. Maximum confidentiality. Understood?"

"Y-yes!"

# 105: They’ve Really Picked “Exciting” Opponents!

Sunday's release time is at 11 EST.

-------

Two quiet days passed, and finally, the long Academy Festival holiday came to an end.

Students and teachers alike returned to campus, gearing up for the upcoming midterms. But Steven hadn't returned, as he was one of the participants in the "Youth Exhibition Match."

At the Kanaz City Pokémon Academy training grounds…

"Ah, the school's training grounds really are nice and peaceful."

Under the shade of a tree, Johan sipped his coffee while holding a book.

Rumble—

The ground trembled slightly, a long furrow forming as Vulpix poked her head up from the soil.

"Oowu?"

With a shake, she leapt out of the ground, scattering dust and bits of snow as her muddy fur shimmered back into its usual, pristine white.

But as Vulpix emerged, another head squeezed its way out from underground.

"Ka-kah~~"

Haunter, clearly too big for the tunnel Vulpix had dug, somehow managed to twist and turn his way out. Then—

"Chu~ Chu~"

A small pink hand wiggled from the tunnel, seemingly stuck.

Vulpix hurriedly grabbed the little hand with her tail, pulling up Tinkatink, who was holding a metallic stone with a proud grin.

"Chu! (Heehee!)"

Covered in dust, Tinkatink plopped onto the ground, looking down at the metallic stone in her hands and beaming with pride.

It was their prize this time—a stone full of precious metal minerals!

Johan's eye twitched as he watched the scene, swallowing the words he had been about to say.

This was the result of a "new venture" Haunter, Vulpix, and Tinkatink had been working on lately.

Tinkatink would use her hammer—now infused with Nosepass's magnetite—to search large areas for metallic traces.

Haunter would use his abilities to pass through walls and objects, narrowing down the location precisely.

And Vulpix would then dig the tunnel, using the "Dig" move she'd just learned two days ago.

With the three Pokémon working in sync, they were now quite skilled at finding and extracting metal ores from the ground.

Even Johan had to admit it: each one of his Pokémon was… incredibly talented.

In fact, he had one thought he wasn't sure he should even say.

With Haunter's uncanny ability to phase through walls, Vulpix's tunneling expertise, and Tinkatink's knack for "borrowing" shiny objects…

They were practically a trio of "outlaws!"

But he hadn't stopped them from this little mining operation, mainly because, in a way, it lightened his own burden. At least for the time being, Tinkatink wouldn't need as many metal ores, easing his expenses a bit.

After a familiar round of "ping-ping-pang-pang" hammering sounds, Tinkatink stood proudly in the center of the training ground, hoisting her hammer like a champion with a trophy.

"Time for a break," Johan called out.

The little ones cheered immediately.

Break time meant treats! They trotted over to him, tails and all, while Johan pulled out the Pokéblocks he had prepared.

Every time he saw them gobbling up Pokéblocks, Johan felt grateful that he'd learned to make them himself. Otherwise, with how much these three ate, his expenses would skyrocket.

While they were engrossed in their snacks, Johan noticed Tinkatink's hammer sitting nearby.

Come to think of it, despite Tinkatink hammering in all sorts of metals every day, her hammer hadn't actually grown any bigger.

Curious, Johan grabbed the hammer's handle.

He gave it a little tug.

Hm?

It didn't budge.

Was it really this heavy?

Gritting his teeth, Johan grasped the handle with both hands, bracing his feet against the ground and pulling with all his strength.

The hammer didn't move at all.

A bead of sweat formed on Johan's forehead.

Tinkatink had shown remarkable strength even from birth, but now Johan was beginning to lose all sense of her limits.

Looking at her small, stubby arms… could she really be this strong?

"Chu?"

Noticing Johan, Tinkatink grinned and lifted the hammer with ease, swinging it around playfully before resting it on her shoulder.

Johan: "…"

He couldn't help but feel like Joseph Stone might have left a few details out when he gifted him Tinkatink's egg.

Did she have some hidden "Herculean Strength" trait?

Or was it… all that practice from smashing Vulpix's snowmen every day?

After all, with Vulpix's current strength, those snowmen shouldn't be so easy to destroy…

Then he remembered something else.

Whenever he held Tinkatink, she actually felt quite light—lighter than when he used to carry Vulpix, even.

Could it be that…

Tinkatink's hammer had a "Meow Hammer" effect?

Once they'd finished eating, Johan got ready to take them to buy some groceries before heading home to pack.

Tomorrow was the opening day of the "Youth Exhibition Match." Conveniently, he didn't have any classes, so if he woke up early, he could catch a flight and arrive just in time for the opening.

In Hoenn's Sootopolis City…

The city's structure resembled a massive volcanic crater—high walls surrounding a central low point.

Crystals scattered throughout the city reflected the sunlight, casting colorful shimmers like rainbows. This natural beauty had earned Sootopolis the title of "Most Beautiful City in Hoenn."

This reputation was also one of the reasons why Sootopolis was chosen as the venue for the "Youth Exhibition Match."

As for Sootopolis's origin, there were two main theories:

One stated that the city was formed when an undersea volcano erupted, its magma rising above the surface to form the island of Sootopolis. This was the official version and the most widely accepted explanation.

The other theory, however, suggested that a massive meteorite struck the earth a thousand years ago, cracking the ground and releasing natural energy and magma, which eventually created Sootopolis.

The meteorite's energy supposedly triggered a clash between the ancient Pokémon Groudon and Kyogre.

This theory was something the Hoenn League would rather keep under wraps.

Today, Sootopolis was bustling with excitement.

A massive circular arena was surrounded by helicopters capturing aerial shots, and four giant screens formed a ring over the stadium, allowing fans without tickets to watch the matches in real-time.

"Huff—"

Panting slightly, Johan reached the entrance of the arena.

Seeing the long lines, he relaxed a bit.

"Looks like it hasn't started yet. Just made it."

Suddenly, a woman in a black trench coat and a round hat with sunglasses spotted him and approached quickly.

Hm?

Just as Johan wondered who she was, the woman leaned in close and spoke in a low voice.

"Hey, pal. Need tickets?"

She held up a few crumpled-looking tickets, clearly for regular seats.

Johan: "…"

For a second, he almost mistook her for Cynthia!

Just then—

"Did you get the wrong person?"

A familiar voice came from behind, carrying a hint of amused teasing.

Turning around, Johan saw a woman with long, silky blonde hair, wearing an unmistakably wide-brimmed hat and sunglasses—Cynthia.

"Not at all," Johan sighed, rubbing his forehead.

"She doesn't exactly have the same… presence or style as you."

Scalper: "???"

Cynthia chuckled and turned to walk toward the VIP entrance.

Johan quickly followed.

"Stealing customers is one thing, but did you have to 'insult' me too…?" The scalper walked off, covering her face.

"Steven's in the first match, and Lance is in the second. They're getting ready now, so they asked me to come get you," Cynthia explained as they walked through the VIP entrance.

At the ticket check, Johan smartly presented the invitation Cynthia had given him, along with his ticket.

After passing through, they entered the arena.

"Who are their opponents?" Johan asked.

"Steven's opponent is Wallace, and Lance's is Lorelei."

"Hiss—"

Johan couldn't help but wince.

Wow.

The organizers really know how to spice things up, don't they?

Soon, they arrived at a secluded private box with large, circular windows offering a full view of the arena. Inside, there were sofas, tea, and even a fruit platter.

Johan released Vulpix and Tinkatink from their Poké Balls, while Haunter emerged from his shadow.

"Ka-kah~~"

Haunter's eyes lit up as he spotted Cynthia, now sitting on the sofa without her hat and sunglasses.

With a grin, he floated over and started giving her a shoulder massage.

Cynthia chuckled softly.

"Thank you."

"Ka-kah Ka-kah~~"

Then she waved over Vulpix and Tinkatink.

"Oowu~~"

Vulpix immediately leapt into her lap, and Cynthia reached for the fruit platter, handing a piece to Tinkatink.

"Chu~~"

Tinkatink beamed and sat down happily to eat.

Johan: "…"

Are these three even my Pokémon?

"So, who's your opponent?" Johan asked, pretending not to notice.

"Hmm…" Cynthia blinked. "My opponent's an easier one—it's Aaron."

"Well, that's definitely… lighter work," Johan mused.

"Hmm?"

An exhibition match indeed—pure "show."

As the host and the mayor of Sootopolis took the stage for opening remarks, the exhibition match officially began.

Cheers erupted from the audience, filling the circular stadium with excitement.

"Now, let's welcome the first match's contestants!"

"From Kanaz City—Steven Stone!"

"And from right here in Sootopolis—Wallace!"

In an instant—

The electrifying atmosphere washed over the arena.

# 106: Wallace and Sharpedo

As an exhibition match, the goal was to "perform," making the outcome secondary. In fact, the line between winning and losing was intentionally blurred.

Still, perhaps by design, each participant used powerful moves with impressive visual appeal, especially Wallace. Although he was the Sootopolis Gym Leader, he was also trained by Adam the Coordinator, and thus his Pokémon moves had a stylish flair.

But to reduce Wallace to just a "trainer of beauty" would be an understatement. Wallace's battling skills were formidable.

After Steven stepped down from the Hoenn Championship title, it was Wallace who took his place!

Wallace held titles as the Sootopolis Gym Leader, Hoenn League Champion, and a Contest Master, showcasing his impressive abilities.

He and Steven were also very close friends.

In the first round, Steven's Aggron suffered a tough loss to Wallace's Swampert due to the type disadvantage. However, Steven's Skarmory was able to turn the tables with a "Substitute-Toxic" and "Iron Defense Dive" strategy, knocking out both Swampert and Walrein.

Skarmory even managed to set up a Stealth Rock trap that left Wallace's Milotic vulnerable when it entered the field.

When Steven's Shiny Metagross finally squared off against Milotic, it secured a clear victory.

Steven's edge over Wallace was undeniable. But Skarmory's tactics captured everyone's attention, reminding many of Johan's battle against Lance, indirectly adding to Johan's growing popularity.

After the match, both Steven and Wallace received thunderous applause from the audience, and they shook hands at the center of the arena, stepping offstage amidst the cheers.

The next match was Lance versus Lorelei.

Now, that was going to be an interesting face-off.

If Steven and Wallace's battle was a friendly spar between pals, Lance and Lorelei's was more… complicated.

To put it simply—

Lance: "Old lady, hello Auntie."

Lorelei: "Oh, look, the 'Dragon Master' using Flying-type Pokémon."

For some reason, Lance, normally calm and composed, turned into a bit of a brat around Lorelei. It was like those mischievous boys in school who pull a girl's braids just to annoy her.

As for Lorelei, the "Ice Queen," even she seemed a little rattled when dealing with Lance.

"Professor Johan."

Just as the match between Lance and Lorelei was getting underway, Steven and Wallace returned.

"This is the friend I wanted to introduce you to, Professor—Wallace, the Sootopolis Gym Leader," Steven said.

Johan had to break out of "spectator mode" for a moment and gave Wallace a warm handshake.

"I've heard much about you—the 'Artist of Water,' Gym Leader Wallace of Sootopolis," Johan said.

Wallace smiled, a warm and welcoming expression on his face. "Professor Johan, the tactics you taught Steven… well, he really beat me up with them."

Despite the words, Wallace didn't seem the least bit angry or disheartened.

"Actually," he continued, "Steven has mentioned you to me more than once. I've been hoping to consult with you but could never find the time with Gym responsibilities…"

"Oh?"

Johan initially thought this was just a bit of polite chit-chat.

To his surprise, Wallace actually reached for a Poké Ball.

"Would you mind taking a look at one of my Pokémon?" he asked.

Johan glanced around; the room was spacious enough, so he nodded. "Sure."

With a smile, Wallace released his Pokémon, cautioning, "It might be a bit rowdy… maybe even aggressive."

As the red light dimmed, a sharp, brutal-looking Pokémon covered in rough blue skin appeared in the room.

Sharpedo!

Sharpedo, the Water/Dark-type "Brutal Pokémon," was known for its violent nature and cunning. It was said that its teeth could bite through steel plates.

However, it also had a humorous nickname as the "Water-type that can't swim."

Seeing Sharpedo, Johan's brow furrowed.

This Sharpedo… it had an unusual number of scars.

True to Wallace's warning, the moment Sharpedo materialized, it appeared highly agitated. Its red eyes darted around, locking onto Johan with a fierce gleam.

"Rrrrawr!!"

Its razor-sharp teeth glinted darkly.

A Bite attack!

Wallace's expression shifted as he readied the Poké Ball to withdraw Sharpedo, calling out, "Sharpedo!"

"Ka-kah!"

Sensing the threat, Haunter, who had been lounging on the glass and watching the match with excitement, instantly materialized in front of Johan, a dark orb forming in its claws.

At the same time, Vulpix leapt out of Cynthia's lap. Though unfamiliar with Wallace, and visibly frightened by Sharpedo's menacing appearance, she braced herself to attack.

Tinkatink, meanwhile, reacted the most directly.

"Chu!!"

With a swing of her hammer, Tinkatink sprang from the sofa, aiming straight for Sharpedo's head.

Johan didn't even have time to stop her.

Sharpedo had dismissed the small Pokémon, figuring she was too small to bother with.

What a joke—she was so small, she wouldn't even be a bite-sized snack.

BAM!!

But as soon as Tinkatink's hammer connected with its head, Sharpedo knew it had made a mistake.

And a big one.

With a tremendous force, accompanied by a scattering of ice crystals, Sharpedo hit the ground hard.

Thud!

A large lump formed on its head.

"Raaawr…"

Tinkatink looked at the quivering Sharpedo, tilting her head in confusion, her lavender eyes filled with questions.

"Chu?"

Did her "Hammer Nap" fail?

She inspected her hammer and then looked down at her hand.

Her expression turned serious.

It must be… that her strength was too low.

Johan quickly scooped her up, stopping her before she could go for another swing. He signaled to Haunter and Vulpix to stand down as well.

Steven and Wallace, who had witnessed the entire episode, now looked at Tinkatink in stunned amazement.

Was this little one's strength a bit… extreme?

Steven was sure Tinkatink had only recently hatched.

Cynthia, on the other hand, wasn't surprised.

If Steven and Wallace knew how Tinkatink trained, they wouldn't be shocked either.

Tinkatink loved smashing things, so Johan arranged for her to hammer away at the sturdy snowmen Vulpix crafted every day.

This kind of training, blending passion with exercise, was the best way to maximize a Pokémon's potential.

Of course, Tinkatink also had an impressive natural strength.

Still, potential alone wasn't enough; it was the trainer's approach that made all the difference.

Sharpedo, eyes tearing up, suddenly felt something odd in its mouth and spat it out.

It was… a tooth!

"Rraaaw! Σ(дlll)"

Clink!

Tinkatink spotted it immediately. She jumped down from Johan's arms, stomped on the tooth, then casually struck a dramatic pose, looking up at the ceiling and whistling innocently.

"Chu (whistles)…"

Johan, Steven, Wallace, and Sharpedo: "…"

Did she really think that tiny foot could hide such a massive tooth?

Did she think people were fools?

Johan covered his face, picking up Tinkatink with an embarrassed smile.

"Ah… I'm very sorry," he said sincerely.

"Chu."

Tinkatink chimed in with her own apology.

Wallace seemed even more embarrassed, waving his hand dismissively.

"No, it's my fault. I thought it was ready to interact with people… I didn't realize it was still so wary. And it's currently in the middle of its tooth-shedding season, too…"

Sharpedo, glancing at Haunter, Tinkatink, and Vulpix, straightened up, nodding earnestly.

That's right—it was shedding season!

Tinkatink had merely helped knock out a tooth that was due to fall out anyway. Perfect!

Johan handed Tinkatink over to Haunter, gesturing for him to take her away.

With a chuckle, Haunter swallowed the Shadow Ball he'd been holding and led Tinkatink away.

From afar, their conversation drifted back to them.

"Chu?" (Why didn't my 'Hammer Nap' work?)

"Ka-kah~~" (That guy's tough; it's normal. Plus, you're still small, not strong enough yet.)

"Chu…" (So… is it okay?)

"Ka-kah~~" (It's fine. It tried to hurt us first! If it does it again, we'll knock out all its teeth!)

"Chu Chu!"

Sharpedo: "…"

Wallace turned to Steven again.

Earlier… did Haunter actually swallow the Shadow Ball?

Steven could only smile; he didn't have an explanation either.

These were Pokémon raised by Professor Johan. Each one unique and impressive, Wallace thought to himself.

He hadn't missed the Vulpix now back in Cynthia's lap either.

"Milotic, Life Dew."

Just then, Cynthia spoke up.

"Milooo~~"

Her Milotic materialized, waving its tail as a gentle stream of water surrounded Sharpedo. The revitalizing aura eased Sharpedo's pain immediately.

Even the missing tooth had already begun to grow back.

It really was shedding season, after all.

"This Milotic…" Wallace murmured with surprise, gazing at Cynthia's Milotic.

"Milooo~~"

Milotic immediately

recognized Johan, approaching him to nuzzle affectionately, then turned its gaze on Sharpedo, eyes blazing.

This time, Sharpedo completely surrendered.

Johan, who had seen Sharpedo's reaction when it prepared to use Bite, received a prompt in his Pokédex.

[Sharpedo]

Type: Water/DarkAbility: Rough SkinKnown Moves: Slash, Feint Attack, Aqua Jet, Leer…Mood: Defensive, Timid (Previously attacked by humans for its dorsal fin)Status: Excellent (Apart from some head pain, all is well)Friendliness: 5 (previously -30)

A timid Sharpedo? That was unusual.

After seeing the reason for its timidity, Johan finally understood why Wallace had sought him out.

At one point, Sharpedo in Hoenn had been hunted nearly to extinction due to overfishing. It was only after the League enacted the Pokémon Protection Act that their population began to recover.

As Johan studied Sharpedo with a serious expression, Wallace couldn't help but ask, "Professor Johan… Sharpedo, is it…?"

Johan stepped forward, to Wallace's surprise, and placed a gentle hand on Sharpedo's head.

"Don't worry."

[Friendliness: 15 (It senses your kindness)]

# 107: Haunter: The Struggles of an Old Soul

Inside the stadium.

"Cloyster, Shell Smash, then Icicle Crash!"

[Shell Smash: Lowers the user's Defense and Special Defense while greatly boosting its Attack, Special Attack, and Speed.]

A true all-or-nothing move.

Lorelei adjusted her glasses, coolly giving the command.

Vulpix, Haunter, and Tinkatink were pressed up against the glass.

At first, Vulpix had hoped to pick up some new Ice-type techniques, but when she saw Cloyster's Shell Smash transformation, she couldn't help but call out.

"Oowu!"

Hey, Big Brother Haunter—isn't that your relative?

"Chu?"

Tinkatink, still young, hadn't yet seen Gastly, so she didn't quite get Vulpix's comment, but her face still showed a look of awe.

Haunter's expression turned complicated, and with a wistful sigh, he replied in a world-weary tone.

"Ka-kah~~"

Don't even bring it up. That was part of my long-gone youth…

Once upon a time, he could transform freely into his own version of "Gastly Cloyster."

Meanwhile, Dragonite, looking tired and worn, steeled herself against the massive icicles forming in the air above.

With a dramatic flourish, Lance swung his cape, his voice ringing with confidence.

"Dragonite, Dragon Rush!"

In the shocked gaze of the crowd, Dragonite, shrouded in a light blue flame, charged headfirst into the enormous, rapidly descending icicles.

Haunter quickly turned to Johan, who was watching from the sidelines, shouting excitedly.

"Ka-kah!!"

Quick, look! Lance is totally cheating again!

Just as Johan and the others came over, Dragonite was facing the full force of a quadruple-weakness Ice-type move from Cloyster, who had boosted with Shell Smash. Despite it all, Dragonite smashed straight through the icicles, scattering them in a flurry of shards before barreling into Cloyster, whose shell was left broken open.

Boom!

Johan's face was a mix of confusion and disbelief.

The whole scene reminded him of an epic, if improbable, martial arts duel. Who would've thought this "Master of Countering Weaknesses" could withstand quadruple weaknesses so recklessly?

"Chu."

Haunter and Vulpix were handling it well, but Tinkatink's big eyes sparkled as she watched.

What a… bold approach to battle!

In the end, Lance's audacious Dragon Rush secured his victory.

It was notable that, throughout the match, Lorelei hadn't used Lapras, and Lance had kept Charizard out of play. The two seemed to have a silent agreement.

"Lance's strength is as remarkable as ever," Steven couldn't help but comment.

"Lorelei's no slouch, either," Wallace agreed, respecting both Trainers' prowess.

Even when facing her type advantage, Lance's sheer skill had the upper hand. But this match also hinted that with Lorelei as an obstacle, his dream of blazing through the Elite Four and winning the Champion title would be nearly impossible.

"If Cloyster had the 'Skill Link' ability and used Icicle Spear, the outcome might have been different," Johan murmured.

"What was that?"

Steven and Wallace didn't catch his words.

Johan shook his head, not elaborating.

At that moment, Cynthia stepped forward.

"It's about time for me to go on."

Her match against Bertha was up next.

"Good luck."

Johan offered his support.

Cynthia gave a soft smile. "Don't worry."

Was it their… ninth or tenth match?

It didn't really matter.

"Go for a three-to-zero win!"

Johan teased.

"Hmm?"

Cynthia blinked and then, passing Wallace on her way to the exit, she added, "Oh, Wallace, by the way—if you're wondering why my Milotic is a bit different from yours, you might want to ask Johan about it."

With that, she exited the VIP box, her voice echoing softly as she moved down the corridor.

"A three-to-zero sweep, huh?"

Meanwhile, somewhere in the other corridor, Bertha shivered unexpectedly.

"Strange…"

"Professor Johan, Miss Cynthia mentioned her Milotic…"

After getting Johan's assurance to help his timid, human-averse Sharpedo, Wallace noticed a difference between his own Milotic and Cynthia's.

Though they looked nearly identical on the surface—and Wallace's Milotic might even seem a little more refined due to different grooming practices—Cynthia's Milotic had an undeniable aura of confidence, radiating from within.

That was something Wallace's Milotic, no matter how polished, couldn't quite match.

Curious, he finally asked.

Johan guessed the question and replied, "Your Milotic evolved with a Prism Scale, right?"

"Wait… did Cynthia's Milotic not evolve using a Prism Scale?"

Wallace's eyes widened, catching the implication.

Both he and his mentor, Adam, had Milotics that evolved using a Prism Scale.

While he knew it was theoretically possible for Feebas to evolve naturally, no one had ever documented a reliable way to do it.

"Yes."

Johan nodded.

That's the difference between a Milotic evolved by external means and one that evolved naturally: an inner self-confidence that flows from the inside out.

"It's not unfixable, but it may take a little more time," Johan added.

Wallace's face lit up with joy.

"Really?"

"Long-term mental exercises would work fine for Milotic, but for Sharpedo, I'd suggest psychological and physical exercises combined for quicker results."

Johan's expertise was clear, and Wallace was happy to follow his advice.

"Rrrawr?"

Sharpedo, sitting in the corner, blinked in surprise.

Was this "physical exercise" the kind of "exercise" it was thinking of?

[Friendliness: 10 (It has concerns about the future...)]

Hmm?

Noticing the change in Sharpedo's status, Johan glanced over.

Sharpedo gave a nervous start, flashing a toothy grin in what it hoped was a friendly smile.

"Rrrawwr~~"

[Friendliness: 20 (It's hopeful about the future!)]

You know, with this attitude alone, Johan thought, Sharpedo was destined for greatness.

"Ka-kah!!"

Johan, come quick—she's about to pull off a three-to-zero sweep!

Haunter suddenly shouted.

Hmm?

Johan and the others looked over at the arena, where they could feel the fierce, fiery energy radiating from the field. Garchomp and Infernape were facing off on the battlefield.

"Is she really going for a three-to-zero?"

Even Steven was taken aback.

In the Pokémon world, bonds and trust weren't just concepts—they were tangible powers.

Take Metagross and Steven, Charizard and Leon, or Garchomp and Cynthia…

In Sinnoh, Cynthia was known as the unparalleled Trainer of her generation, largely thanks to Garchomp.

Not that Bertha and her Infernape were weak, but… even before Garchomp's final evolution, she had consistently bested Bertha's Infernape.

With Garchomp's evolved power and the additional type advantage, Cynthia held a clear edge.

Johan sometimes thought Garchomp might even be able to take on her whole team alone if she wanted to.

"Infernape, we're not giving up yet—get up!" Bertha's voice was charged with intensity, her eyes ablaze.

Infernape's flames roared to life in response.

Its Blaze ability!

This ability is perfect for Trainers who draw out strength through emotion.

But against Cynthia's Garchomp…

"Garchomp, Earthquake to finish!"

"Roooaaarrr!!"

Garchomp threw her arms wide and bellowed skyward, her body glowing with white light and a metallic edge, before stomping down with immense force.

Rumble!

The entire battlefield trembled as if the ground itself was breaking apart. Even though Infernape tried to leap away, it couldn't avoid the rocks shaking loose in the aftermath of Garchomp's attack.

The combo of Swords Dance and Earthquake left a tremendous impact.

"Infernape is unable to battle! The winner is Cynthia from Sinnoh!"

Cheers erupted throughout the stadium.

Bertha felt defeated, close to tears.

She wasn't sure why Cynthia seemed more charged up than usual; it was rare to see her so fired up.

Today felt like…

Everyone else was showing off their skills while Bertha was the one taking the hits.

Still, losing to Cynthia wasn't something that bothered Bertha too much.

Each loss just strengthened her resolve to keep pushing forward.

Haunter, meanwhile, had somehow found three sets of pom-poms and passed them to Vulpix and Tinkatink.

Grabbing Johan, he led his two companions straight for the competitor's exit tunnel.

"Where are you going?"

Johan looked bewildered.

Haunter's face twisted into a look of dismay at Johan's naiveté.

"Ka-kah!"

We're going to congratulate her! Don't you have any common sense?

Johan: "???"

Without waiting for his response, Haunter pushed him forward.

Internally, Haunter sighed, "Why do I even have to remind him? When will Johan ever grow up? I'm just always worrying."

In the tunnel:

"Ka-kah~ Ka-kah~"

"Oowu~ Oowu~"

"Chu~ Chu~"

With Haunter in the lead, Vulpix followed shyly but kept the rhythm, and T

inkatink looked puzzled but amused, swaying along.

Johan, meanwhile, had his face in his hands, leaning against the wall.

The social awkwardness was killing him…

"Ka-kah!!"

Seeing Cynthia approach amid the applause, Haunter led the little group forward, his pom-poms moving in a quick rhythm.

Vulpix breathed a small puff of ice to form a flower, presenting it with pride.

Cynthia blinked, then a warm smile, as radiant as a rainbow, spread across her face.

"Thank you."

When she spotted Johan standing awkwardly to the side, her smile widened.

"Professor Johan, you're such a kind soul. Your Pokémon's love for you shines through, as clear and pure as glass."

Watching as Johan was coaxed away, Wallace couldn't help but marvel.

Johan had only just met Sharpedo, yet it had let him touch its head so soon…

That natural gift for connecting with Pokémon was rare, and Wallace felt his own talents paled in comparison.

"Yes,"

Steven nodded in agreement.

"It's not just Wallace's Pokémon—mine respect him greatly, too."

Metagross, Skarmory… both had accepted Johan without hesitation.

"It's because Professor Johan treats every Pokémon with genuine care. But we mustn't take his help for granted." Steven's tone was serious.

Wallace smiled knowingly.

"Don't worry."

He understood Steven's implicit warning well.

"We'll make sure not to disappoint Professor Johan."

# 108: I’m Really Not in This for the Money

"Are you sure this is okay?"

Johan looked at the Energy Cube Recipe Book in his hands, hesitating.

The book was a clear gift from Wallace.

In the Pokémon world, breeders and coordinators alike are often experts at making high-quality Pokéblocks. Of course, it's not just Pokéblocks they're skilled in.

For breeders, their craft goes beyond making Pokéblocks to include breeding and raising Pokémon, massage therapy, postnatal care, and more.

As for coordinators, they specialize in raising Pokémon for contests, with an emphasis not only on moves but also on a Pokémon's physical beauty and poise, enhancing every aspect of its appeal.

So, making Pokéblocks is just one skill both coordinators and breeders hone as part of their work.

Under the guidance of Adam, the esteemed coordinator, Wallace had honed his craft to the highest level. Even Steven often sourced his Pokéblocks from Wallace.

This recipe book, incorporating Adam's and Wallace's combined techniques for Pokéblock-making, was immensely valuable.

Wallace smiled warmly.

"I heard from Steven that you also make Pokéblocks for your Pokémon. These might be useful."

Useful? That was an understatement…

Before Johan could respond, Wallace continued.

"My mentor always said that we shouldn't hoard our knowledge selfishly. When someone truly needs your help, don't withhold what you know. Besides…"

"Johan, you went out of your way to help me without hesitation."

Hearing this, Johan glanced back down at the recipe book.

To be honest, this book was more valuable than many TMs or high-tier items.

"Well, if you're sure… then thank you. It really will help a lot."

The better the Pokéblocks, the better for him and his Pokémon.

"Some recipes focus on enhancing appearance rather than stats," Wallace noted. "I've modified some, but others I haven't. You might want to experiment a bit."

The Youth Exhibition Match was still ongoing, with notable battles drawing the crowd's attention. But for Johan's group, their focus had shifted.

A notification flashed on Johan's phone, and he frowned slightly.

[Dragon Envoy]: I've got something to take care of, so I'm heading out first.

Was Lance… dodging Lorelei?

Few people could intimidate him, but Lorelei was evidently one of them.

Meanwhile, Cynthia mentioned that Diantha had to leave right after her battle, urged on by her manager's packed schedule.

Just then, Johan received another message.

[Forever 18]: Are you still in Hoenn? [Grain in the Field]: Yes, I'm in Sootopolis City.

[Forever 18]: I'm at Sootopolis too, on 3rd Street, next to the Wailord Plush Shop at the Sealeo Ice Cream Parlor…

Johan rubbed his chin thoughtfully.

Why did he feel like he might know this person?

"What's up?"

Cynthia asked, noticing his expression as she shaped Vulpix's fruit plate to resemble a Milotic.

"You're pretty good at this," Johan remarked.

"An online 'client' of mine also happens to be in Sootopolis and wants to meet up."

Cynthia tilted her head, intrigued.

"Want me to come along?"

Thinking of Smoochum's situation, perhaps a Milotic sighting could be the boost it needed, so Johan nodded.

"Actually, that might be good."

Sootopolis, built like a giant volcanic crater, had high outer walls sloping down to its center. Seawater streamed through the city's canals, adding to its charm and easy travel options.

Together, Johan and Cynthia took one of these iconic boat rides, heading to "Forever 18's" location on 3rd Street.

The Wailord Plush Shop was well-known, its towering Wailord sign visible from a distance.

At the Sealeo Ice Cream Parlor, Cynthia, Haunter, and the Pokémon were immediately captivated by the vibrant display of treats in the window.

"This looks delicious…"

Cynthia's eyes sparkled as she looked at Johan.

Johan, busy sending an "I'm here" message, responded automatically.

"Then get some."

Then he noticed everyone's expectant looks fixed squarely on him.

All right.

The Pokémon didn't have money, and Cynthia… well, Cynthia might take hours to decide.

Johan stepped forward, scanning the menu.

"Two Walrein Jumbo Sundaes, five Sealeo Scoops…"

Bzzt—

A Pokéball at Cynthia's waist popped open, and her Eevee bounced out.

"Eevee!"

"...Let's make that six Sealeo Scoops."

Just then…

"Professor Johan and… Cynthia?"

A slightly surprised voice sounded from the side.

There stood Lorelei, casually holding an ice cream while awkwardly hiding a Wailord plush behind her back, eyes darting between the two of them.

"Lorelei?"

"Lorelei?"

Johan and Cynthia exchanged looks, each equally surprised.

Then Johan's face shifted, realizing something.

Lance, Lorelei, Steven, Wallace, Cynthia, Diantha… so this Youth Exhibition Match had become a Patient Showcase?

They practically made up half the participant list.

Under a patio umbrella outside the Sealeo Ice Cream Parlor, Johan, Cynthia, and Lorelei sat around a table.

Haunter was thoroughly enjoying his two Walrein Jumbos, grinning mischievously as he licked each in turn.

Meanwhile, Tinkatink, Eevee, and Vulpix sat on the table with their Sealeo Scoops.

"So cute…"

Lorelei murmured, admiring the Pokémon. She quickly collected herself, clearing her throat as she summoned her Smoochum.

"Smooch?"

Smoochum looked around, momentarily lost, before her eyes landed on Vulpix.

"Smooch!"

With a squeal of delight, Smoochum hopped onto the table, wrapping her tiny arms around Vulpix and pressing her face to Vulpix's fur, nuzzling contentedly.

"Smooch~~ Smooch~~"

Vulpix—hee hee, it's Vulpix!

Seeing Smoochum's plump lips puckering up for a kiss, Vulpix's eyes went wide in horror.

"O-owoo! Σ(дlll)"

She kicked her legs frantically, trying to shake Smoochum off, but Smoochum was undeterred, determined to give her idol a smooch.

Haunter's eyes widened in shock.

This one's bolder than me…

Lorelei covered her face.

Johan could barely watch.

He'd thought Smoochum would see Vulpix as a rival—but here she was, starstruck.

Meeting her online client really was a good idea; some things just didn't translate well online.

Cynthia, however, found the scene delightful.

"Looks like Smoochum really admires Vulpix."

Seeing that Vulpix was getting "overwhelmed," Tinkatink discreetly reached for her hammer, her gaze narrowing on Smoochum.

"Chu?"

Head or rump… probably head, right?

Noticing this, Johan felt a sudden chill.

One hit to Smoochum's head, and it'd be game over…

Fortunately, Lorelei reacted quickly, recalling her overly affectionate Smoochum with a sheepish apology.

But she felt that letting Smoochum meet her "idol" might inspire her to train even harder.

They chatted for a while, with Johan offering Lorelei some final suggestions for Smoochum's progress.

When Cynthia casually mentioned that Lance had already left Sootopolis, Lorelei's demeanor instantly shifted to a more serious tone.

After hurriedly excusing herself, she handed Johan a small box as a thank-you gift and then dashed off.

"Hm?"

Watching Lorelei hurry away, Cynthia tilted her head.

"Are Lorelei and Lance… not on the best terms?"

Wait, now you're noticing?

"Not exactly. There's a saying, 'Teasing shows affection; true love kicks harder.' Lance and Lorelei are a special case," Johan said, laughing as he opened the box.

Inside was a Never-Melt Ice crystal!

[Never-Melt Ice: Boosts the power of Ice-type moves when held by a Pokémon.]

This was an expensive item.

And the quality of Lorelei's crystal was top-notch, likely worth over a million.

Johan took a deep breath.

Lorelei came from Island Four of the Sevii Islands, where the famed Icefall Cave produced Never-Melt Ice. That alone was probably worth more than several of Steven's mines.

These people were all loaded.

All Johan wanted was to offer some free guidance online for Trainers and Pokémon—yet he kept getting showered with unexpected "gifts."

It's just…

I'm really not in this for the money.

He pocketed the Never-Melt Ice and the box.

"What about Clair?"

Cynthia asked, genuinely curious and maybe… just a little intrigued.

Ah, so even Cynthia has a bit of a gossipy side, Johan mused.

"Don't worry. Lance is a high-flyer and can handle it," he said, grinning.

If Lance was tough, wait till Red got here—he'd break hearts but leave no trace.

"Oh?"

Cynthia blinked, sensing a deeper meaning behind Johan's words.

Suddenly, Cynthia smiled slyly and, with a hint of playfulness, asked,

"And what about you?"
